Types, algorithms and test names below are\n14: planned interfaces, not implemented behavior.\n15: \n16: ## 1. Architecturally Significant Requirements\n17: \n18: | Requirement | Acceptance criteria that shape this decision | Why they matter architecturally |\n19: |---|---|---|\n20: | REQ-001 | C-001 | A code-created specification must cross the supervisor/researcher boundary before execution. |\n21: | REQ-001 | C-002, C-003, M-001 | Code derives status from question-linked findings and task-local retrieved sources; model prose cannot establish coverage. |\n22: | REQ-001 | C-004 | Invalid output has a typed failure path rather than an unvalidated summary. |\n23: | REQ-003 | C-001, C-002, M-002 | Model completion is a request; a deterministic coverage check controls exit. |\n24: | REQ-003 | C-003, C-004, C-005, M-001 | Progress, iteration bounds and supervisor errors share one ordered exit selector. |\n25: | REQ-003 | C-006 | Two historical queries require later live evidence; offline design arguments cannot prove this criterion. |\n26: | REQ-002, contributor | C-005, C-007 | Valid unrun delegations receive results, and failure/placeholder prose cannot become report findings. Dispatch, queuing and joining remain delivery's responsibility. |\n27: \n28: Relevant proposed invariants: INV-003, INV-006 and INV-007. The complete case\n29: inventory is the orchestration section of `verification-constraints.md` §7.\n30: \n31: ## 2. Context\n32: \n33: At the baseline, `state.py::ConductResearch` has only `research_topic`; researcher\n34: output is free text. `supervisor_tools` exits before dispatch on model completion,\n35: empty tool calls or `research_iterations > max_researcher_iterations`\n36: (`deep_researcher.py:240–262`). Results above the concurrency slice are lost and a\n37: child exception ends the phase (`:288–342`, delivery's area). `researcher` increments\n38: a model-turn counter (`:422`), while `researcher_tools` checks the limit only after\n39: executing a whole tool batch (`:473–498`). `compress_research` returns unstructured\n40: text or a placeholder (`:511–585`). `utils.get_notes_from_tool_calls` collects tool\n41: text without an evidence boundary (`utils.py:599–601`).\n42: \n43: `state.py::override_reducer` **appends ordinary lists**; it replaces a value only\n44: for `{\"type\": \"override\", \"value\": ...}`. A full cumulative list returned as an\n45: ordinary delta would duplicate old entries. `ResearcherOutputState` is a separate\n46: output schema, so a new internal field alone would not reach delivery.\n47: \n48: The project requires thin existing nodes, unchanged graph topology and extension\n49: modules importing shared types. `state.py` and `configuration.py` must not import\n50: orchestration. Python remains 3.12; no dependency or permission change is proposed.\n51: \n52: ## 3. Decision\n53: \n54: Choose EVAL-002 option B: shared types in `state.py`, with pure contract, execution\n55: evidence and termination helpers in `orchestration/`. Existing nodes call those\n56: helpers. Keep delivery's fan-out/join and context's trimming algorithms with their\n57: owners. Every behavior below is behind `orchestration_enabled`, default `False`.\n58: \n59: ### 3.1 Accepting delegations\n60: \n61: The enabled schema is `ConductResearchV2`, exposed to the model under the unchanged\n62: tool name **ConductResearch**. Keep the original class for the disabled path.\n63: \n64: Validate raw arguments before dispatch. Normalize question identity with\n65: `\" \".join(text.split()).casefold()`, preserving the first original question for\n66: messages and unanswered lists. Remove duplicates in first-occurrence order.\n67: An empty question list falls back to the nonblank topic. More than five distinct\n68: questions, blank/non-string entries, a blank topic or a wrong field type are\n69: validation errors, not silently truncated tasks. Missing scope defaults to `\"\"`.\n70: Assign `T1`, `T2`, … to accepted specifications in original call order.\n71: \n72: Record every raw call by `tool_call_id` in `delegation_receipts`. A malformed raw\n73: intent receives a `rejected` diagnostic and a matching validation-error\n74: ToolMessage, is never dispatched, and contributes no invented question to coverage.\n75: Only an accepted valid TaskSpec is a delegated research task. Every accepted task\n76: must have exactly one TaskResult, including accepted-but-unrun tasks. This explicit\n77: interpretation of malformed intent requires delivery/owner review; it is not a\n78: silent exception or a change to approved requirement text.\n79: \n80: Replaying a known call ID with identical arguments reuses its registration/result;\n81: different arguments for that ID are a protocol error. A completed task is never\n82: converted into `not_run`. This bookkeeping prevents duplicate *state* entries; it\n83: does not promise exactly-once external side effects across a process crash.\n84: \n85: ### 3.2 Actual execution budget and trusted evidence\n86: \n87: `TaskSpec.max_tool_calls` is a maximum of **started executable tool invocations**,\n88: not model turns, HTTP requests inside a tool, search queries inside one invocation,\n89: or token cost. Initially use the configured `max_react_tool_calls` as that maximum.\n90: It must be an integer at least one; retain the original model-turn watchdog as an\n91: additional bound, with its existing value and disabled-path semantics.\n92: \n93: Before creating concurrent execution awaitables, admit executable calls in model\n94: call order, reserving at most the remaining permits. Each started invocation costs\n95: one permit, including a tool that raises and `think_tool`. A denied call costs zero\n96: and receives a matching budget-denied response. `ResearchComplete` is a control\n97: signal with no executable invocation, so costs zero; resolve all other calls in a\n98: mixed batch before compression. Once permits are exhausted, start no more tools\n99: and proceed to compression after all admitted calls join.\n100: \n101: Each accepted task has a code-owned `TaskExecutionEvidence` receipt. The task\n102: wrapper retains it and passes it through a fresh per-task copy of RunnableConfig's\n103: private configurable key `_orchestration_evidence`. Do not mutate shared config,\n104: forward this key into model configuration, or include the live object in prompts.\n105: It records call IDs/starts, trusted returned URLs and available usage metadata.\n106: State stores only serializable `task_evidence_snapshot` checkpoints. A wrapper that\n107: catches a child exception can therefore preserve already-spent tool calls instead\n108: of falsely returning zero. Missing usage metadata is recorded as incomplete in the\n109: receipt; token counters sum **reported** usage and are not claimed as billing totals.\n110: \n111: Only successful retrieval receipts matched to admitted tool IDs can add HTTP(S)\n112: URLs. Tavily's returned source fields/anchored source entries and explicitly\n113: supported MCP metadata adapters are eligible. `think_tool` echoes, error strings,\n114: model messages, arbitrary prose URLs and an output's self-declared `retrieved_urls`\n115: are ineligible. Unknown MCP result shapes add no trusted URLs; do not guess.\n116: Use exact returned URL strings, with no query/fragment removal or redirect\n117: equivalence. The ledger survives message trimming and does not rely on `raw_notes`.\n118: \n119: Native OpenAI/Anthropic search executes inside model calls and lacks an audited\n120: strict per-task admission/count contract in this baseline. The proposed enabled\n121: path rejects those search modes before any research model request, with an\n122: explicit compatibility error; it never substitutes a provider. This restriction\n123: needs search/delivery review. Flag-off retains the existing provider behavior.\n124: No native search or live project run was performed during this SA task.\n125: \n126: ### 3.3 Compression draft protocol and result acceptance\n127: \n128: Preserve context ADR-005's `CompressionOutcome.summary: str`. On the enabled path,\n129: the existing compression request produces ordinary Markdown with exactly one\n130: sidecar **before** the final, unique `### Sources` section:\n131: \n132: ````text\n133: <task_findings_v1>\n134: ```json\n135: {\"schema_version\":1,\"findings\":[{\"claim\":\"A claim\",\"source_numbers\":[1],\"answers\":[\"Exact required question\"]}]}\n136: ```\n137: </task_findings_v1>\n138: ### Sources\n139: [1] Source title: https://example.org/evidence\n140: ````\n141: \n142: The delimiters must be standalone lines, there must be exactly one block and one\n143: JSON object, and the block allows only the shown keys. Require integer version 1,\n144: nonblank claims, positive integer source numbers (booleans excluded), and answer\n145: strings exactly matching that task's original `must_answer` items. Parse sources\n146: only as `[n] Title: HTTP(S)-URL` lines in the final section; identifiers are unique,\n147: sequential from one and URLs are unique. Titles are display text, not authority.\n148: \n149: Missing/duplicate delimiters, malformed JSON, extra keys, wrong types, unknown\n150: question/source references or ambiguous source numbering fail the task as\n151: `invalid_result`. A structurally valid finding that refers to a URL absent from\n152: the trusted task receipt is discarded; derive status from the remaining findings.\n153: This distinction implements C-003 without treating all invalid output alike.\n154: \n155: Code builds TaskResult's identity, questions, cost, retrieved URLs, coverage and\n156: status. The model only proposes claims/answer links/source references. The\n157: compressor adapter strips the sidecar from `compressed_research` and its internal\n158: summary before returning to delivery; the raw context string cannot bypass this\n159: adapter into a ConductResearch ToolMessage. Malformed results return no summary.\n160: \n161: At finalization, construct `notes` deterministically from validated Finding.claim\n162: and only their referenced validated sources, in task/finding order. Never copy\n163: free-text summary, raw notes, the sidecar, reflections or failure diagnostics into\n164: report findings. Findings retain per-task numbered citations and one Sources list\n165: per task; final cross-task renumbering remains citations/report prompt's job.\n166: URL membership establishes retrieval provenance, **not semantic entailment**.\n167: \n168: ### 3.4 Coverage, turns and one exit selector\n169: \n170: Let Q be the distinct normalized required questions of all accepted registered\n171: tasks, including failed and not_run tasks. A question is answered if a validated\n172: finding in any task answers it. Coverage is `|answered| / |Q|`, or zero for empty Q.\n173: Use exact fractions internally; float fields are serialization only. Re-delegating\n174: the same question does not increase the denominator. New unanswered questions can\n175: lower coverage. Require nonempty Q and a finite threshold `0 < threshold <= 1`\n176: before covered; proposed default `coverage_threshold = 0.8` needs SA Review.\n177: \n178: `completed_supervisor_turns` starts at zero. It counts successfully processed\n179: supervisor responses, including think-only, no-tool and completion-only turns.\n180: At tools entry, register all raw intents before evaluating an early exit. If the\n181: count is already N=`max_researcher_iterations`, accepted pending tasks are not\n182: dispatched and receive `failed/not_run`. Otherwise process the turn, join every\n183: started task, then increment the count and evaluate exit. At supervisor entry an\n184: already reached cap finalizes without a new model request. Thus N=1 allows one\n185: processed delegation turn; N=2 allows at most two. Keep the old `research_iterations`\n186: counter and comparisons confined to flag-off or diagnostic compatibility.\n187: \n188: For an eligible processed turn, compare coverage immediately before and after the\n189: turn using its prior and new registered ledgers. Equal coverage increments\n190: `unchanged_eligible_turns`; any change resets it to zero. Ignore think-only turns\n191: for this streak, without resetting it; they still consume the global turn budget.\n192: No-tool, completion-only and mixed turns are eligible. Two unchanged eligible\n193: turns with a think-only turn between them therefore stall. Registering new\n194: questions is part of the after-turn denominator, not evidence of progress by itself.\n195: \n196: One pure `select_exit` chooses, in this order:\n197: \n198: 1. **covered:** nonempty Q and coverage meets threshold;\n199: 2. **budget_exhausted:** processed-turn cap reached;\n200: 3. **stalled:** two unchanged eligible turns;\n201: 4. **failed:** supervisor's own model call exhausted its existing configured retries;\n202: 5. otherwise continue.\n203: \n204: At a supervisor error, pass the error to this same selector, using current state;\n205: do not hardcode failed and overwrite a higher-priority condition. Child failures\n206: become task results through delivery and do not independently end research.\n207: ResearchComplete and no-tool turns are requests, never completion authority. If\n208: the loop continues, return unresolved original questions in deterministic order.\n209: Tool calls receive matching ToolMessages; no-tool feedback uses a HumanMessage,\n210: not a fabricated tool-call ID. Mixed completion/delegation turns process all valid\n211: delegations unless an already applicable early exit prevents dispatch.\n212: \n213: Before any early exit, resolve every accepted pending registration to its actual\n214: result or not_run, then recompute Q/coverage and select the final reason. Example:\n215: old 4/5 coverage plus two distinct pending unanswered questions becomes 4/7;\n216: at cap it exits budget_exhausted. Repeating the same questions leaves 4/5 and\n217: covered wins. Never reuse a stale `run_coverage` value.\n218: \n219: ## 4. Rejected Options\n220: \n221: | Option | Reason for rejection |\n222: |---|---|\n223: | Prompt-only gating | Cannot establish code-derived status, provenance or exit authority; fails critical drivers. |\n224: | Whole-node orchestration takeover | Can be correct, but absorbs delivery's dispatch/join scope and maximizes shared-node coordination. |\n225: \n226: The full qualitative comparison is in EVAL-002. No prototype, latency measurement\n227: or passing behavior test is claimed by this selection.\n228: \n229: ## 5. Components and Interfaces\n230: \n231: ### Shared types, defined once in `state.py`\n232: \n233: | Type | Fields / constraints |\n234: |---|---|\n235: | ConductResearchV2 | `research_topic: str`, `must_answer: list[str]=[]`, `scope: str=\"\"`; validation per §3.1; exported tool name ConductResearch. |\n236: | TaskSpec | `task_id: str`, `research_topic: str`, `must_answer: list[str]`, `scope: str`, `max_tool_calls: int`; code-created, nonempty valid questions and budget. |\n237: | Source | `source_id: str` (S1…), `url: str`, `title: str=\"\"`; ID unique within task. |\n238: | Finding | `claim: str`, `source_ids: list[str]`, `answers: list[str]`; nonempty valid sources, exact task question references. |\n239: | TaskCost | `tool_calls: int=0`, `input_tokens: int=0`, `output_tokens: int=0`; strict nonnegative integers, no boolean coercion. |\n240: | TaskResult | `task_id`, `status`, optional `failure_category/failure_reason`, `summary=\"\"`, `findings=[]`, `sources=[]`, `retrieved_urls=[]`, `must_answer`, `coverage=0.0`, `cost`; fresh collection defaults. |\n241: | RunRecord | `exit_reason`, optional `exit_detail`, `run_coverage`, `coverage_threshold`, `task_results=[]`; final snapshot only. |\n242: | TaskExecutionEvidence / SerializableReceipt | Private code-owned live execution receipt and its serializable snapshots; no model-authored authority fields. |\n243: \n244: Enums remain v0.5's `complete|partial|failed`,\n245: `covered|budget_exhausted|stalled|failed`, and\n246: `unanswered|invalid_result|runtime|token_limit|not_run`.\n247: Complete requires coverage 1 and no failure fields; partial requires 0<coverage<1\n248: and unanswered; failed requires coverage 0. Every noncomplete result has category\n249: and reason. Runtime/token_limit/invalid_result/not_run failures have no findings or\n250: summary; unanswered may retain an internal summary but never contributes report\n251: notes without validated findings. `unanswered()` preserves question order.\n252: Reject nonfinite coverage/threshold, duplicate IDs, identity/question mismatches,\n253: and claimed cost/provenance inconsistent with the code receipt.\n254: \n255: | Component | Responsibility | Interface (planned) | Called by |\n256: |---|---|---|---|\n257: | contracts.py | Valid specs, draft parsing, result validation and failure construction | `build_task_spec(tool_call, task_id: str, config) -> TaskSpec`; `render_task_message(spec) -> str` | delivery dispatch; thin nodes |\n258: | contracts.py | Build trusted results | `build_task_result(spec, compression_text: str, *, evidence) -> TaskResult`; `validate_task_result(output, spec, *, evidence) -> TaskResult` | compression adapter; delivery collector |\n259: | contracts.py | Typed failures with actual cost | `failed_result(spec, category, message, *, evidence) -> TaskResult`; `not_run_result(spec) -> TaskResult` | delivery/context exception mapping; cap finalization |\n260: | evidence.py | Admit executable calls, record receipts and supported source metadata, snapshot evidence | `admit_tool_calls(calls, spec, evidence)`; `record_tool_result(evidence, call_id, observation, adapter)`; `validate_execution_capability(config) -> None` | researcher / researcher_tools; orchestration entry |\n261: | termination.py | Registration, exact coverage, progress and exit selection | `register_delegations(calls, receipts, config)`; `coverage_snapshot(results)`; `select_exit(snapshot, *, cap_reached, unchanged_turns, supervisor_error)` | supervisor_tools; supervisor error/entry |\n262: | termination.py | Validate completeness and produce one terminal update | `finalize_run(receipts, results, selection, threshold) -> RunRecord`; `RunRecord.to_state_update() -> dict` | thin node finalization |\n263: | state.py | Read final output | `RunRecord.from_state(state) -> RunRecord | None` | delivery and any final-state reader |\n264: \n265: `from_state` returns None for an absent run (empty/absent results and all terminal\n266: fields absent/None), raises ValueError for an incomplete/inconsistent final\n267: snapshot, and is **not** used on intermediate supervisor checkpoints. Reader\n268: failure policy belongs to delivery. `to_state_update` emits all five run fields\n269: together plus explicit override envelopes for task_results and notes. Finalizers\n270: check registration/result cardinality as well as the model's structural validators.\n271: \n272: Shared readers must review this contract: Alchemy (delivery), Tekon/BroZhai\n273: (context), and the citations owner for the compression protocol. Evaluation's\n274: current draft consumes report/brief/evidence rather than requiring a new\n275: orchestration import; preserve those inputs without introducing a dependency.\n276: \n277: ## 6. State, Failure Handling and Deterministic Boundary\n278: \n279: - **State:** the orchestration section of memory-design §5 owns the field table,\n280:   defaults, writer and lifecycle. New supervisor-private counters/receipts and\n281:   researcher evidence snapshots must not become AgentState output fields.\n282: - **Runtime policies:** POL-002–POL-010, detailed in the module policy section.\n283: - **Invariants:** INV-003, INV-006 and INV-007, all Proposed.\n284: - **Deterministic boundary:** input acceptance, execution permits, source admission,\n285:   result validation/status, coverage, progress and exit selection do not call a\n286:   model. Model proposals enter these boundaries as untrusted data.\n287: \n288: Delivery returns each turn's ordered **plain list delta** via\n289: `update_payload[\"task_results\"]`. The node computes old+delta transiently, then\n290: returns the delta once when continuing. On exit it returns the final override\n291: instead of both delta and override. No extension directly mutates graph state;\n292: the node is the single channel writer. The existing `write_research_brief` return\n293: initializes a new enabled run: clear own terminal fields/results and private\n294: counters/receipts. Direct supervisor/researcher entry requires a fresh run input\n295: or an explicitly resumed checkpoint; never infer a new run merely from a None\n296: exit_reason. Flag-off writes none of the new fields.\n297: \n298: ### Per-turn context and compaction contract (course D2.2)\n299: \n300: | Call | Ordered assembly | Protected / re-injected authority |\n301: |---|---|---|\n302: | Supervisor | system instructions and tool schema → immutable research brief → conversation/tool results → deterministic unresolved-question feedback | Completion rule, cap and unresolved original question texts are supplied by code, not copied from retrieved text. |\n303: | Researcher | research system instruction → first HumanMessage from TaskSpec → AI/tool history | Topic, scope, exact questions and tool budget remain in the protected preamble; tool observations remain untrusted data. |\n304: | Compression, every attempt | system compression/citation instructions → immutable TaskSpec/evidence reference block → surviving history → final compression instruction with sidecar rules | Rebuild spec, trusted source-ID/URL map and format constraints after trimming. The ledger itself stays outside mutable messages. Insert JSON/schema fragments **after** existing `.format(...)`, avoiding accidental format braces. |\n305: \n306: The baseline output caps are research 10,000 tokens, compression 8,192, report\n307: 10,000 and Tavily summarization 8,192. They are configurable **output** maxima;\n308: `max_content_length=50,000` is a **character** cap. Neither is an input-token cap.\n309: `get_model_token_limit` is a static lookup, may return None, and does not enforce\n310: request input size. Do not present it as a verified provider context window.\n311: \n312: The current bounded compaction trigger is a recognized compression token-limit\n313: error, classified against **compression_model**, followed by context ADR-005's\n314: oldest-whole-turn recovery, at most three attempts. For on/on there is one recovery\n315: loop, not nested three-by-three retries. Context must preserve the preamble,\n316: compression instruction and newest AI/tool turn. Orchestration re-injects critical\n317: spec/format/source references for every attempt; it does not implement trimming.\n318: \n319: **Integration gap:** a proactive input-token cap/compaction threshold with an\n320: approved tokenizer/model-window source is not defined in current main or the\n321: context draft. Course D2.2 requires the whole-system policy. Ask Tekon/Alchemy to\n322: resolve it at integration; this ADR supplies the boundary and reactive behavior,\n323: not a fabricated numerical input cap or an unapproved tokenizer dependency.\n324: \n325: Flag combinations: off/off keeps baseline; on/off uses typed acceptance and the\n326: bounded baseline compression attempts; on/on uses context's one recovery loop\n327: followed by this parser; off/on has no TaskSpec and needs context/delivery to agree\n328: their typed-exception mapping. Do not invent orchestration fields for off/on or\n329: claim it is baseline-equivalent when context is enabled.\n330: \n331: ## 7. Verification\n332: \n333: Every REQ-001 C-001–C-004/M-001 and REQ-003 C-001–C-006/M-001–M-002 maps to its\n334: exact requirement-specified path in verification-constraints §7. Additional\n335: planned cases cover budget admission before fan-out, failed-call cost, forged\n336: provenance, sidecar parsing/stripping, stable duplicate questions, threshold\n337: boundaries, denominator growth, precedence, N=1/N=2, idempotent registration,\n338: delta/override behavior, new-run reset and flags. Each proposed invariant has\n339: named violation tests. Run the shared contract suite and every existing offline\n340: suite after implementation; do not author those tests before Gate 1.\n341: \n342: REQ-003 C-006 is **NOT_RUN**. The recovered historical query texts and the old\n343: search-disabled configuration are recorded in the raw evidence reference. A\n344: source-capable live configuration must be reviewed before claiming sourced\n345: coverage; preserve query text and disclose configuration differences. No Claude\n346: API is permitted by this student's task authorization.\n347: \n348: Actual SA checks, their commands and verdicts are recorded in the review checklist.\n349: Document checks do not establish runtime correctness, live fit or human approval.\n350: \n351: ## 8. Consequences\n352: \n353: **Positive:** code decides authoritative status/exit; trusted evidence survives\n354: trimming; each accepted task is traceable; readers have one terminal snapshot;\n355: the extension can be tested with fake models and literal fixtures.\n356: \n357: **Costs:** several existing node hooks and shared state additions require coordinated\n358: review. Strict parsing can reject useful but malformed model output. Findings\n359: inherit the model's question/claim association: URL provenance is narrower than\n360: fact verification. Coverage is of delegated questions, not proof that the model\n361: decomposed the entire research brief correctly. Native-search compatibility is\n362: narrowed on the enabled path until strict budget support is designed.\n363: \n364: **Operational limits:** receipt state deduplication is not durable exactly-once\n365: tool execution. A crash after an external request but before checkpointing can\n366: leave uncertain cost/side effects; do not automatically replay that window or\n367: claim a verified result. Diagnostics must avoid credentials and stack traces in\n368: reader-facing reasons; original safe error text/category remains available to\n369: delivery. No new retention service or cross-version checkpoint migration is added.\n370: \n371: Reopen this decision when any shared field/helper or citation protocol changes;\n372: native search gains auditable pre-request caps; receipt/ledger invariants fail;\n373: input-budget policy cannot protect the declared context layers; real compression\n374: frequently fails the strict schema; live C-006 is unmet; a requirement changes; or\n375: checkpoint/resume needs stronger crash guarantees. Reopen through ADD/ADR and SA\n376: Review, with affected tests returning to Gate 2; never weaken tests to fit output.\n377: \n378: ## 9. Implementation Steps\n379: \n380: These are future Build steps **after Gate 1, Test Author baseline evidence and\n381: Gate 2**. No step below has been performed in this documentation branch.\n382: \n383: 1. In `state.py`, define the shared types and validators once; add own fields to\n384:    the existing state classes and task_result to ResearcherOutputState. Leave\n385:    baseline ConductResearch and existing reducer behavior intact.\n386: 2. Add `orchestration/__init__.py`, `contracts.py`, `evidence.py`, `termination.py`.\n387:    Implement the pure algorithms in §3 and the interfaces in §5. Add the new\n388:    subpackage to `[tool.setuptools] packages` in `pyproject.toml` with human review;\n389:    this is packaging, not a new dependency or permission change.\n390: 3. Add only `orchestration_enabled=False` and validated `coverage_threshold=0.8`\n391:    to Configuration with `x_oap_ui_config`. Add enabled-run initialization to\n392:    `write_research_brief`'s existing return. Specify fresh versus resumed direct\n393:    subgraph inputs in the public helper documentation and fixtures.\n394: 4. In `supervisor`, select the enabled tool schema, check the processed-turn cap\n395:    before another request and map exhausted configured model retries through the\n396:    same exit selector. Add no new model client and no unbounded retry loop.\n397: 5. In `supervisor_tools`, register intents once, apply pre-dispatch exits, and use\n398:    delivery's approved wrapper for task-scoped evidence, queuing, isolation and\n399:    join. **Alchemy owns dispatch/gather/consumer error handling.** Centay supplies\n400:    contract/registration helpers and the pre/post-turn selector. Agree the hunk\n401:    split before editing; context's proposed change to the missing-summary default\n402:    is the same delivery hunk, not a second owner.\n403: 6. In `researcher`/`researcher_tools`, validate enabled execution capability, carry\n404:    the protected TaskSpec, allocate permits before task creation, capture matching\n405:    receipts and maintain serializable snapshots. Keep baseline tool assembly at\n406:    `get_all_tools`; do not change provider selection or search fallback policy.\n407: 7. In `compress_research`, call exactly one selected recovery path. On success\n408:    parse/strip its string response and build TaskResult with the trusted receipt;\n409:    on typed context exhaustion or other failure use the evidence-aware helper.\n410:    **Tekon owns trimming/classification/recovery**, Centay the successful result\n411:    adapter and the common exhausted-result mapping, Alchemy exception collection.\n412: 8. Draft enabled-only prompt additions in `prompts.py`, reusing the citations\n413:    owner's format and avoiding format-brace collisions. Jeremy/citations and\n414:    Tekon review the added protected instructions. Keep the original disabled\n415:    strings and existing graph topology; record prompt version/evaluation in the\n416:    integrator's approved scheme rather than inventing a version-file migration.\n417: 9. At every finalization, verify all accepted registrations have one result, apply\n418:    final precedence, emit all terminal fields together, override results/notes,\n419:    and keep internal counters/receipts out of main output. Delivery alone computes\n420:    run outcome and limitations from that snapshot.\n421: 10. Run the approved contract/new/all-prior offline suites and active project\n422:     gates; Verify independently records real results. Run the approved live queries\n423:     on an authorized non-Claude configuration only after the required reviews.\n424:     Update code/test evidence in trace-matrix and the actual AI-use/review records;\n425:     never mark the requirement verified from this SA draft.","title":"read","metadata":{"metadata":{"truncated":false},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read file /Users/e1bae/src/homework/5351/project/sa-centay-2026-11-14/docs/architecture/adr/002-orchestration-contract-and-termination.md, lines 1-425\n1: # ADR-002: Typed research tasks and deterministic coverage termination\n2: \n3: - **Status:** Proposed\n4: - **Date:** 2026-11-14\n5: - **Module:** orchestration\n6: - **Owner:** Centay — XIE Shentai, 61061965\n7: - **Evaluation:** EVAL-002 (`docs/architecture/evaluation-table.md`)\n8: - **SA Review:** pending; no Gate 1 or Gate 2 approval exists.\n9: \n10: This is a local SA proposal against `827fd32f956a0601df3e1fd253d5cc98044fff88`.\n11: It supersedes neither approved requirements nor another owner's draft. Differences\n12: from the historical v0.5 field discussion and reader decisions are listed in\n13: `../orchestration-review-checklist.md`. Types, algorithms and test names below are\n14: planned interfaces, not implemented behavior.\n15: \n16: ## 1. Architecturally Significant Requirements\n17: \n18: | Requirement | Acceptance criteria that shape this decision | Why they matter architecturally |\n19: |---|---|---|\n20: | REQ-001 | C-001 | A code-created specification must cross the supervisor/researcher boundary before execution. |\n21: | REQ-001 | C-002, C-003, M-001 | Code derives status from question-linked findings and task-local retrieved sources; model prose cannot establish coverage. |\n22: | REQ-001 | C-004 | Invalid output has a typed failure path rather than an unvalidated summary. |\n23: | REQ-003 | C-001, C-002, M-002 | Model completion is a request; a deterministic coverage check controls exit. |\n24: | REQ-003 | C-003, C-004, C-005, M-001 | Progress, iteration bounds and supervisor errors share one ordered exit selector. |\n25: | REQ-003 | C-006 | Two historical queries require later live evidence; offline design arguments cannot prove this criterion. |\n26: | REQ-002, contributor | C-005, C-007 | Valid unrun delegations receive results, and failure/placeholder prose cannot become report findings. Dispatch, queuing and joining remain delivery's responsibility. |\n27: \n28: Relevant proposed invariants: INV-003, INV-006 and INV-007. The complete case\n29: inventory is the orchestration section of `verification-constraints.md` §7.\n30: \n31: ## 2. Context\n32: \n33: At the baseline, `state.py::ConductResearch` has only `research_topic`; researcher\n34: output is free text. `supervisor_tools` exits before dispatch on model completion,\n35: empty tool calls or `research_iterations > max_researcher_iterations`\n36: (`deep_researcher.py:240–262`). Results above the concurrency slice are lost and a\n37: child exception ends the phase (`:288–342`, delivery's area). `researcher` increments\n38: a model-turn counter (`:422`), while `researcher_tools` checks the limit only after\n39: executing a whole tool batch (`:473–498`). `compress_research` returns unstructured\n40: text or a placeholder (`:511–585`). `utils.get_notes_from_tool_calls` collects tool\n41: text without an evidence boundary (`utils.py:599–601`).\n42: \n43: `state.py::override_reducer` **appends ordinary lists**; it replaces a value only\n44: for `{\"type\": \"override\", \"value\": ...}`. A full cumulative list returned as an\n45: ordinary delta would duplicate old entries. `ResearcherOutputState` is a separate\n46: output schema, so a new internal field alone would not reach delivery.\n47: \n48: The project requires thin existing nodes, unchanged graph topology and extension\n49: modules importing shared types. `state.py` and `configuration.py` must not import\n50: orchestration. Python remains 3.12; no dependency or permission change is proposed.\n51: \n52: ## 3. Decision\n53: \n54: Choose EVAL-002 option B: shared types in `state.py`, with pure contract, execution\n55: evidence and termination helpers in `orchestration/`. Existing nodes call those\n56: helpers. Keep delivery's fan-out/join and context's trimming algorithms with their\n57: owners. Every behavior below is behind `orchestration_enabled`, default `False`.\n58: \n59: ### 3.1 Accepting delegations\n60: \n61: The enabled schema is `ConductResearchV2`, exposed to the model under the unchanged\n62: tool name **ConductResearch**. Keep the original class for the disabled path.\n63: \n64: Validate raw arguments before dispatch. Normalize question identity with\n65: `\" \".join(text.split()).casefold()`, preserving the first original question for\n66: messages and unanswered lists. Remove duplicates in first-occurrence order.\n67: An empty question list falls back to the nonblank topic. More than five distinct\n68: questions, blank/non-string entries, a blank topic or a wrong field type are\n69: validation errors, not silently truncated tasks. Missing scope defaults to `\"\"`.\n70: Assign `T1`, `T2`, … to accepted specifications in original call order.\n71: \n72: Record every raw call by `tool_call_id` in `delegation_receipts`. A malformed raw\n73: intent receives a `rejected` diagnostic and a matching validation-error\n74: ToolMessage, is never dispatched, and contributes no invented question to coverage.\n75: Only an accepted valid TaskSpec is a delegated research task. Every accepted task\n76: must have exactly one TaskResult, including accepted-but-unrun tasks. This explicit\n77: interpretation of malformed intent requires delivery/owner review; it is not a\n78: silent exception or a change to approved requirement text.\n79: \n80: Replaying a known call ID with identical arguments reuses its registration/result;\n81: different arguments for that ID are a protocol error. A completed task is never\n82: converted into `not_run`. This bookkeeping prevents duplicate *state* entries; it\n83: does not promise exactly-once external side effects across a process crash.\n84: \n85: ### 3.2 Actual execution budget and trusted evidence\n86: \n87: `TaskSpec.max_tool_calls` is a maximum of **started executable tool invocations**,\n88: not model turns, HTTP requests inside a tool, search queries inside one invocation,\n89: or token cost. Initially use the configured `max_react_tool_calls` as that maximum.\n90: It must be an integer at least one; retain the original model-turn watchdog as an\n91: additional bound, with its existing value and disabled-path semantics.\n92: \n93: Before creating concurrent execution awaitables, admit executable calls in model\n94: call order, reserving at most the remaining permits. Each started invocation costs\n95: one permit, including a tool that raises and `think_tool`. A denied call costs zero\n96: and receives a matching budget-denied response. `ResearchComplete` is a control\n97: signal with no executable invocation, so costs zero; resolve all other calls in a\n98: mixed batch before compression. Once permits are exhausted, start no more tools\n99: and proceed to compression after all admitted calls join.\n100: \n101: Each accepted task has a code-owned `TaskExecutionEvidence` receipt. The task\n102: wrapper retains it and passes it through a fresh per-task copy of RunnableConfig's\n103: private configurable key `_orchestration_evidence`. Do not mutate shared config,\n104: forward this key into model configuration, or include the live object in prompts.\n105: It records call IDs/starts, trusted returned URLs and available usage metadata.\n106: State stores only serializable `task_evidence_snapshot` checkpoints. A wrapper that\n107: catches a child exception can therefore preserve already-spent tool calls instead\n108: of falsely returning zero. Missing usage metadata is recorded as incomplete in the\n109: receipt; token counters sum **reported** usage and are not claimed as billing totals.\n110: \n111: Only successful retrieval receipts matched to admitted tool IDs can add HTTP(S)\n112: URLs. Tavily's returned source fields/anchored source entries and explicitly\n113: supported MCP metadata adapters are eligible. `think_tool` echoes, error strings,\n114: model messages, arbitrary prose URLs and an output's self-declared `retrieved_urls`\n115: are ineligible. Unknown MCP result shapes add no trusted URLs; do not guess.\n116: Use exact returned URL strings, with no query/fragment removal or redirect\n117: equivalence. The ledger survives message trimming and does not rely on `raw_notes`.\n118: \n119: Native OpenAI/Anthropic search executes inside model calls and lacks an audited\n120: strict per-task admission/count contract in this baseline. The proposed enabled\n121: path rejects those search modes before any research model request, with an\n122: explicit compatibility error; it never substitutes a provider. This restriction\n123: needs search/delivery review. Flag-off retains the existing provider behavior.\n124: No native search or live project run was performed during this SA task.\n125: \n126: ### 3.3 Compression draft protocol and result acceptance\n127: \n128: Preserve context ADR-005's `CompressionOutcome.summary: str`. On the enabled path,\n129: the existing compression request produces ordinary Markdown with exactly one\n130: sidecar **before** the final, unique `### Sources` section:\n131: \n132: ````text\n133: <task_findings_v1>\n134: ```json\n135: {\"schema_version\":1,\"findings\":[{\"claim\":\"A claim\",\"source_numbers\":[1],\"answers\":[\"Exact required question\"]}]}\n136: ```\n137: </task_findings_v1>\n138: ### Sources\n139: [1] Source title: https://example.org/evidence\n140: ````\n141: \n142: The delimiters must be standalone lines, there must be exactly one block and one\n143: JSON object, and the block allows only the shown keys. Require integer version 1,\n144: nonblank claims, positive integer source numbers (booleans excluded), and answer\n145: strings exactly matching that task's original `must_answer` items. Parse sources\n146: only as `[n] Title: HTTP(S)-URL` lines in the final section; identifiers are unique,\n147: sequential from one and URLs are unique. Titles are display text, not authority.\n148: \n149: Missing/duplicate delimiters, malformed JSON, extra keys, wrong types, unknown\n150: question/source references or ambiguous source numbering fail the task as\n151: `invalid_result`. A structurally valid finding that refers to a URL absent from\n152: the trusted task receipt is discarded; derive status from the remaining findings.\n153: This distinction implements C-003 without treating all invalid output alike.\n154: \n155: Code builds TaskResult's identity, questions, cost, retrieved URLs, coverage and\n156: status. The model only proposes claims/answer links/source references. The\n157: compressor adapter strips the sidecar from `compressed_research` and its internal\n158: summary before returning to delivery; the raw context string cannot bypass this\n159: adapter into a ConductResearch ToolMessage. Malformed results return no summary.\n160: \n161: At finalization, construct `notes` deterministically from validated Finding.claim\n162: and only their referenced validated sources, in task/finding order. Never copy\n163: free-text summary, raw notes, the sidecar, reflections or failure diagnostics into\n164: report findings. Findings retain per-task numbered citations and one Sources list\n165: per task; final cross-task renumbering remains citations/report prompt's job.\n166: URL membership establishes retrieval provenance, **not semantic entailment**.\n167: \n168: ### 3.4 Coverage, turns and one exit selector\n169: \n170: Let Q be the distinct normalized required questions of all accepted registered\n171: tasks, including failed and not_run tasks. A question is answered if a validated\n172: finding in any task answers it. Coverage is `|answered| / |Q|`, or zero for empty Q.\n173: Use exact fractions internally; float fields are serialization only. Re-delegating\n174: the same question does not increase the denominator. New unanswered questions can\n175: lower coverage. Require nonempty Q and a finite threshold `0 < threshold <= 1`\n176: before covered; proposed default `coverage_threshold = 0.8` needs SA Review.\n177: \n178: `completed_supervisor_turns` starts at zero. It counts successfully processed\n179: supervisor responses, including think-only, no-tool and completion-only turns.\n180: At tools entry, register all raw intents before evaluating an early exit. If the\n181: count is already N=`max_researcher_iterations`, accepted pending tasks are not\n182: dispatched and receive `failed/not_run`. Otherwise process the turn, join every\n183: started task, then increment the count and evaluate exit. At supervisor entry an\n184: already reached cap finalizes without a new model request. Thus N=1 allows one\n185: processed delegation turn; N=2 allows at most two. Keep the old `research_iterations`\n186: counter and comparisons confined to flag-off or diagnostic compatibility.\n187: \n188: For an eligible processed turn, compare coverage immediately before and after the\n189: turn using its prior and new registered ledgers. Equal coverage increments\n190: `unchanged_eligible_turns`; any change resets it to zero. Ignore think-only turns\n191: for this streak, without resetting it; they still consume the global turn budget.\n192: No-tool, completion-only and mixed turns are eligible. Two unchanged eligible\n193: turns with a think-only turn between them therefore stall. Registering new\n194: questions is part of the after-turn denominator, not evidence of progress by itself.\n195: \n196: One pure `select_exit` chooses, in this order:\n197: \n198: 1. **covered:** nonempty Q and coverage meets threshold;\n199: 2. **budget_exhausted:** processed-turn cap reached;\n200: 3. **stalled:** two unchanged eligible turns;\n201: 4. **failed:** supervisor's own model call exhausted its existing configured retries;\n202: 5. otherwise continue.\n203: \n204: At a supervisor error, pass the error to this same selector, using current state;\n205: do not hardcode failed and overwrite a higher-priority condition. Child failures\n206: become task results through delivery and do not independently end research.\n207: ResearchComplete and no-tool turns are requests, never completion authority. If\n208: the loop continues, return unresolved original questions in deterministic order.\n209: Tool calls receive matching ToolMessages; no-tool feedback uses a HumanMessage,\n210: not a fabricated tool-call ID. Mixed completion/delegation turns process all valid\n211: delegations unless an already applicable early exit prevents dispatch.\n212: \n213: Before any early exit, resolve every accepted pending registration to its actual\n214: result or not_run, then recompute Q/coverage and select the final reason. Example:\n215: old 4/5 coverage plus two distinct pending unanswered questions becomes 4/7;\n216: at cap it exits budget_exhausted. Repeating the same questions leaves 4/5 and\n217: covered wins. Never reuse a stale `run_coverage` value.\n218: \n219: ## 4. Rejected Options\n220: \n221: | Option | Reason for rejection |\n222: |---|---|\n223: | Prompt-only gating | Cannot establish code-derived status, provenance or exit authority; fails critical drivers. |\n224: | Whole-node orchestration takeover | Can be correct, but absorbs delivery's dispatch/join scope and maximizes shared-node coordination. |\n225: \n226: The full qualitative comparison is in EVAL-002. No prototype, latency measurement\n227: or passing behavior test is claimed by this selection.\n228: \n229: ## 5. Components and Interfaces\n230: \n231: ### Shared types, defined once in `state.py`\n232: \n233: | Type | Fields / constraints |\n234: |---|---|\n235: | ConductResearchV2 | `research_topic: str`, `must_answer: list[str]=[]`, `scope: str=\"\"`; validation per §3.1; exported tool name ConductResearch. |\n236: | TaskSpec | `task_id: str`, `research_topic: str`, `must_answer: list[str]`, `scope: str`, `max_tool_calls: int`; code-created, nonempty valid questions and budget. |\n237: | Source | `source_id: str` (S1…), `url: str`, `title: str=\"\"`; ID unique within task. |\n238: | Finding | `claim: str`, `source_ids: list[str]`, `answers: list[str]`; nonempty valid sources, exact task question references. |\n239: | TaskCost | `tool_calls: int=0`, `input_tokens: int=0`, `output_tokens: int=0`; strict nonnegative integers, no boolean coercion. |\n240: | TaskResult | `task_id`, `status`, optional `failure_category/failure_reason`, `summary=\"\"`, `findings=[]`, `sources=[]`, `retrieved_urls=[]`, `must_answer`, `coverage=0.0`, `cost`; fresh collection defaults. |\n241: | RunRecord | `exit_reason`, optional `exit_detail`, `run_coverage`, `coverage_threshold`, `task_results=[]`; final snapshot only. |\n242: | TaskExecutionEvidence / SerializableReceipt | Private code-owned live execution receipt and its serializable snapshots; no model-authored authority fields. |\n243: \n244: Enums remain v0.5's `complete|partial|failed`,\n245: `covered|budget_exhausted|stalled|failed`, and\n246: `unanswered|invalid_result|runtime|token_limit|not_run`.\n247: Complete requires coverage 1 and no failure fields; partial requires 0<coverage<1\n248: and unanswered; failed requires coverage 0. Every noncomplete result has category\n249: and reason. Runtime/token_limit/invalid_result/not_run failures have no findings or\n250: summary; unanswered may retain an internal summary but never contributes report\n251: notes without validated findings. `unanswered()` preserves question order.\n252: Reject nonfinite coverage/threshold, duplicate IDs, identity/question mismatches,\n253: and claimed cost/provenance inconsistent with the code receipt.\n254: \n255: | Component | Responsibility | Interface (planned) | Called by |\n256: |---|---|---|---|\n257: | contracts.py | Valid specs, draft parsing, result validation and failure construction | `build_task_spec(tool_call, task_id: str, config) -> TaskSpec`; `render_task_message(spec) -> str` | delivery dispatch; thin nodes |\n258: | contracts.py | Build trusted results | `build_task_result(spec, compression_text: str, *, evidence) -> TaskResult`; `validate_task_result(output, spec, *, evidence) -> TaskResult` | compression adapter; delivery collector |\n259: | contracts.py | Typed failures with actual cost | `failed_result(spec, category, message, *, evidence) -> TaskResult`; `not_run_result(spec) -> TaskResult` | delivery/context exception mapping; cap finalization |\n260: | evidence.py | Admit executable calls, record receipts and supported source metadata, snapshot evidence | `admit_tool_calls(calls, spec, evidence)`; `record_tool_result(evidence, call_id, observation, adapter)`; `validate_execution_capability(config) -> None` | researcher / researcher_tools; orchestration entry |\n261: | termination.py | Registration, exact coverage, progress and exit selection | `register_delegations(calls, receipts, config)`; `coverage_snapshot(results)`; `select_exit(snapshot, *, cap_reached, unchanged_turns, supervisor_error)` | supervisor_tools; supervisor error/entry |\n262: | termination.py | Validate completeness and produce one terminal update | `finalize_run(receipts, results, selection, threshold) -> RunRecord`; `RunRecord.to_state_update() -> dict` | thin node finalization |\n263: | state.py | Read final output | `RunRecord.from_state(state) -> RunRecord | None` | delivery and any final-state reader |\n264: \n265: `from_state` returns None for an absent run (empty/absent results and all terminal\n266: fields absent/None), raises ValueError for an incomplete/inconsistent final\n267: snapshot, and is **not** used on intermediate supervisor checkpoints. Reader\n268: failure policy belongs to delivery. `to_state_update` emits all five run fields\n269: together plus explicit override envelopes for task_results and notes. Finalizers\n270: check registration/result cardinality as well as the model's structural validators.\n271: \n272: Shared readers must review this contract: Alchemy (delivery), Tekon/BroZhai\n273: (context), and the citations owner for the compression protocol. Evaluation's\n274: current draft consumes report/brief/evidence rather than requiring a new\n275: orchestration import; preserve those inputs without introducing a dependency.\n276: \n277: ## 6. State, Failure Handling and Deterministic Boundary\n278: \n279: - **State:** the orchestration section of memory-design §5 owns the field table,\n280:   defaults, writer and lifecycle. New supervisor-private counters/receipts and\n281:   researcher evidence snapshots must not become AgentState output fields.\n282: - **Runtime policies:** POL-002–POL-010, detailed in the module policy section.\n283: - **Invariants:** INV-003, INV-006 and INV-007, all Proposed.\n284: - **Deterministic boundary:** input acceptance, execution permits, source admission,\n285:   result validation/status, coverage, progress and exit selection do not call a\n286:   model. Model proposals enter these boundaries as untrusted data.\n287: \n288: Delivery returns each turn's ordered **plain list delta** via\n289: `update_payload[\"task_results\"]`. The node computes old+delta transiently, then\n290: returns the delta once when continuing. On exit it returns the final override\n291: instead of both delta and override. No extension directly mutates graph state;\n292: the node is the single channel writer. The existing `write_research_brief` return\n293: initializes a new enabled run: clear own terminal fields/results and private\n294: counters/receipts. Direct supervisor/researcher entry requires a fresh run input\n295: or an explicitly resumed checkpoint; never infer a new run merely from a None\n296: exit_reason. Flag-off writes none of the new fields.\n297: \n298: ### Per-turn context and compaction contract (course D2.2)\n299: \n300: | Call | Ordered assembly | Protected / re-injected authority |\n301: |---|---|---|\n302: | Supervisor | system instructions and tool schema → immutable research brief → conversation/tool results → deterministic unresolved-question feedback | Completion rule, cap and unresolved original question texts are supplied by code, not copied from retrieved text. |\n303: | Researcher | research system instruction → first HumanMessage from TaskSpec → AI/tool history | Topic, scope, exact questions and tool budget remain in the protected preamble; tool observations remain untrusted data. |\n304: | Compression, every attempt | system compression/citation instructions → immutable TaskSpec/evidence reference block → surviving history → final compression instruction with sidecar rules | Rebuild spec, trusted source-ID/URL map and format constraints after trimming. The ledger itself stays outside mutable messages. Insert JSON/schema fragments **after** existing `.format(...)`, avoiding accidental format braces. |\n305: \n306: The baseline output caps are research 10,000 tokens, compression 8,192, report\n307: 10,000 and Tavily summarization 8,192. They are configurable **output** maxima;\n308: `max_content_length=50,000` is a **character** cap. Neither is an input-token cap.\n309: `get_model_token_limit` is a static lookup, may return None, and does not enforce\n310: request input size. Do not present it as a verified provider context window.\n311: \n312: The current bounded compaction trigger is a recognized compression token-limit\n313: error, classified against **compression_model**, followed by context ADR-005's\n314: oldest-whole-turn recovery, at most three attempts. For on/on there is one recovery\n315: loop, not nested three-by-three retries. Context must preserve the preamble,\n316: compression instruction and newest AI/tool turn. Orchestration re-injects critical\n317: spec/format/source references for every attempt; it does not implement trimming.\n318: \n319: **Integration gap:** a proactive input-token cap/compaction threshold with an\n320: approved tokenizer/model-window source is not defined in current main or the\n321: context draft. Course D2.2 requires the whole-system policy. Ask Tekon/Alchemy to\n322: resolve it at integration; this ADR supplies the boundary and reactive behavior,\n323: not a fabricated numerical input cap or an unapproved tokenizer dependency.\n324: \n325: Flag combinations: off/off keeps baseline; on/off uses typed acceptance and the\n326: bounded baseline compression attempts; on/on uses context's one recovery loop\n327: followed by this parser; off/on has no TaskSpec and needs context/delivery to agree\n328: their typed-exception mapping. Do not invent orchestration fields for off/on or\n329: claim it is baseline-equivalent when context is enabled.\n330: \n331: ## 7. Verification\n332: \n333: Every REQ-001 C-001–C-004/M-001 and REQ-003 C-001–C-006/M-001–M-002 maps to its\n334: exact requirement-specified path in verification-constraints §7. Additional\n335: planned cases cover budget admission before fan-out, failed-call cost, forged\n336: provenance, sidecar parsing/stripping, stable duplicate questions, threshold\n337: boundaries, denominator growth, precedence, N=1/N=2, idempotent registration,\n338: delta/override behavior, new-run reset and flags. Each proposed invariant has\n339: named violation tests. Run the shared contract suite and every existing offline\n340: suite after implementation; do not author those tests before Gate 1.\n341: \n342: REQ-003 C-006 is **NOT_RUN**. The recovered historical query texts and the old\n343: search-disabled configuration are recorded in the raw evidence reference. A\n344: source-capable live configuration must be reviewed before claiming sourced\n345: coverage; preserve query text and disclose configuration differences. No Claude\n346: API is permitted by this student's task authorization.\n347: \n348: Actual SA checks, their commands and verdicts are recorded in the review checklist.\n349: Document checks do not establish runtime correctness, live fit or human approval.\n350: \n351: ## 8. Consequences\n352: \n353: **Positive:** code decides authoritative status/exit; trusted evidence survives\n354: trimming; each accepted task is traceable; readers have one terminal snapshot;\n355: the extension can be tested with fake models and literal fixtures.\n356: \n357: **Costs:** several existing node hooks and shared state additions require coordinated\n358: review. Strict parsing can reject useful but malformed model output. Findings\n359: inherit the model's question/claim association: URL provenance is narrower than\n360: fact verification. Coverage is of delegated questions, not proof that the model\n361: decomposed the entire research brief correctly. Native-search compatibility is\n362: narrowed on the enabled path until strict budget support is designed.\n363: \n364: **Operational limits:** receipt state deduplication is not durable exactly-once\n365: tool execution. A crash after an external request but before checkpointing can\n366: leave uncertain cost/side effects; do not automatically replay that window or\n367: claim a verified result. Diagnostics must avoid credentials and stack traces in\n368: reader-facing reasons; original safe error text/category remains available to\n369: delivery. No new retention service or cross-version checkpoint migration is added.\n370: \n371: Reopen this decision when any shared field/helper or citation protocol changes;\n372: native search gains auditable pre-request caps; receipt/ledger invariants fail;\n373: input-budget policy cannot protect the declared context layers; real compression\n374: frequently fails the strict schema; live C-006 is unmet; a requirement changes; or\n375: checkpoint/resume needs stronger crash guarantees. Reopen through ADD/ADR and SA\n376: Review, with affected tests returning to Gate 2; never weaken tests to fit output.\n377: \n378: ## 9. Implementation Steps\n379: \n380: These are future Build steps **after Gate 1, Test Author baseline evidence and\n381: Gate 2**. No step below has been performed in this documentation branch.\n382: \n383: 1. An explicitly fresh supervisor input initializes its private counters/receipts at the supervisor entry; do not try to store supervisor-private keys in `AgentState` or infer a fresh run merely from `exit_reason is None`. A resume of that same run does not reinitialize them. There is one supervisor run per main invocation; a later invocation must not inherit its predecessor's ledger or progress. The fresh-versus-resumed subgraph input protocol is a required integration test, not a claimed baseline capability.\n68: - Every observed delegation intent is registered in original tool-call order before dispatch or final coverage selection. A valid spec receives the next run-local task ID. Invalid intent, including no valid topic/question or more than five distinct valid questions, produces a rejected diagnostic receipt, without inventing a spec or a coverage question. The definition of an accepted delegation and this diagnostic representation require shared-reader Review.\n69: - A receipt records the original call identity, accepted serialized spec/task ID or rejection diagnostic, and its eventual terminal result reference. Re-observing the same unchanged receipt does not create another task or append another result. A reused ID with different intent is a conflict diagnostic, not a second silently accepted task. An already terminal receipt is not converted to `not_run` on a cap resume.\n70: - Delivery produces dispatch inputs and one ordered result delta; it does not directly mutate graph state or choose an exit. The node's orchestration boundary validates that delta, updates receipts, and emits each new terminal result once. These are event responsibilities at one integration point, not two modules writing the same field.\n71: - While the supervisor runs, its ledger may be nonempty with `exit_reason is None`. This is a valid **running** state. `RunRecord.from_state()` is exclusively a reader of the final main snapshot and rejects an incomplete final snapshot; it is not called on the running supervisor ledger. The five final run fields are published together through one finalization update. Private counters, receipts and evidence snapshots are not main output fields.\n72: - Per-turn `update_payload[\"task_results\"]` is a plain delta: the reducer appends it. The coverage calculation may temporarily inspect `old_results + delta`, but must not return that full list as a plain update. At finalization, both `task_results` and `notes` use `{\"type\": \"override\", \"value\": ...}`. Notes contain only rendered validated claims and their validated source references, never the untrusted complete summary, JSON sidecar, error, placeholder or reflection.\n73: \n74: **Execution evidence and context**\n75: \n76: - Each accepted task gets one live `TaskExecutionEvidence` holder. The dispatch wrapper makes a separate copy of `RunnableConfig` and of its `configurable` mapping, then injects the holder under the runtime-private key `_orchestration_evidence`. Parallel tasks never share this holder. It is passed only to the orchestration execution adapters; strip that private key before constructing model configuration, prompt content or exported diagnostics. Do not put the live holder, locks, permits, callbacks, model clients or awaitables in graph state; state contains serializable snapshots only.\n77: - The wrapper owns the live holder for the entire invocation, including exceptional returns. Before starting local awaitables, it reserves ordered permits within the remaining actual-call budget; it records each started executable invocation, including `think_tool` and failures. Rejected calls and the non-executed `ResearchComplete` control signal spend zero. Factories `build_task_result`, `validate_task_result` and `failed_result` receive the holder through the required keyword-only `evidence` argument, so a model cannot submit authoritative cost or retrieved URLs. `not_run_result(spec)` uses zero-spend evidence by construction.\n78: - Spec, remaining budget, receipts and trusted retrieved URLs survive message trimming. Before every compression attempt, assembly restores the immutable spec/authority and the sidecar rules outside expendable history. A serializable evidence snapshot supports inspection/checkpoint validation, but does not turn a live external tool's side effects into a transaction.\n79: - Checkpoint or trace adapters must exclude the runtime-private holder itself. Receipt deduplication protects local registration/result publication, not cross-process exactly-once tool execution: a crash after an external call but before a terminal receipt is durable leaves a replay window. Safe recovery of an unfinished receipt requires an explicit policy; no automatic side-effect replay guarantee is made here.\n80: - Existing model `*_max_tokens` fields are output limits and `max_content_length` is a character limit. This design supplies no measured input/context-window token cap. Reactive compression token-limit handling, at most three attempts, remains the current trigger; proactive course input-budget compliance is a documented integration gap, not an implemented threshold.\n81: \n82: **Feature flags and shared-reader Review**\n83: \n84: | `orchestration_enabled` | `context_trimming` | Proposed lifecycle |\n85: |---|---|---|\n86: | `False` | `False` | Exact baseline path; no new orchestration fields, injected holder or altered tool binding. |\n87: | `True` | `False` | Task contract/evidence and typed result mapping apply; retain the baseline compression retry/trimming path rather than taking over context policy. Compression success is parsed and failure is mapped using retained evidence. |\n88: | `True` | `True` | Context exclusively owns the single recovery loop, at most three attempts; it returns its existing string outcome. Orchestration parses that string and maps `TokenLimitExceeded` through `failed_result(..., evidence=...)`; no nested retry loop. |\n89: | `False` | `True` | Context's exception can exist without a TaskSpec. Its propagation/delivery behavior is **pending context/delivery Review**; do not manufacture orchestration fields or claim this combination is the baseline. |\n90: \n91: - **Shared contract:** delivery/Alchemy reads or creates results through helpers; context/BroZhai (Tekon) maps compression failure through the same factory; citations/Jeremy reviews the optional machine-readable sidecar and protected prompt placement; evaluation/Doppler reads only final outputs. Reader approval is pending. Native search is rejected before a flag-on model request until strict pre-request tool caps and auditable provenance/counts are agreed; search's existing configuration validator and provider selection stay separate.\n92: - **Planned verification:** `test/offline/test_contracts.py::{test_task_results_reducer_append_and_override,test_run_record_final_snapshot_contract}` and `test/offline/test_orchestration_integration.py::{test_receipt_replay_does_not_duplicate_tasks,test_new_run_resets_private_progress,test_finalization_joins_then_replaces_notes_and_results,test_flag_combinations,test_enabled_context_assembly_preserves_authority}`. delivery/evaluation readers | New-run reset → exactly one selected final reason → next-run reset |\n57: | `SupervisorState`, `AgentState` | `exit_detail` | `str \\| None`, default `None` | last write | Same common run finalizer | Same readers | New-run reset → final explanation, required for non-`covered` → next-run reset |\n58: | `SupervisorState`, `AgentState` | `run_coverage` | `float \\| None`, default `None` | last write | Same common run finalizer, from exact question counts | Same readers | New-run reset → final computed value → next-run reset |\n59: | `SupervisorState`, `AgentState` | `coverage_threshold` | `float \\| None`, default `None` | last write | Same common run finalizer, recording the configured value used for the run | Same readers | New-run reset → final finite value in `(0, 1]` → next-run reset |\n60: | `SupervisorState` only | `completed_supervisor_turns` | `int`, default `0` | last write | Orchestration tool-turn adapter at the end of one successfully processed turn | Entry cap guard and common exit selector | First enabled supervisor entry → increment after processing/join, including think-only/completion-only/no-tool turns → next-run reset |\n61: | `SupervisorState` only | `unchanged_eligible_turns` | `int`, default `0` | last write | Same end-of-turn adapter | Common exit selector | First enabled supervisor entry → increment/reset by exact before/after coverage; think-only leaves it untouched → next-run reset |\n62: | `SupervisorState` only | `delegation_receipts` | Ordered `dict[str, SerializableReceipt]`, default `{}`; key is the run's `tool_call_id` | last write of the complete receipt map | Orchestration registration/result-acceptance boundary within `supervisor_tools` | Delivery dispatch adapter; pending-delegation handling; coverage/finalization validation | First observed delegation intent → rejected diagnostic or accepted spec → terminal result reference → next-run reset |\n63: | Existing `SupervisorState`, `AgentState` field | `notes` | Existing `list[str]` | Existing `override_reducer`; enabled finalization uses an explicit override | Common run finalizer; existing report-generation cleanup remains its later lifecycle event | `final_report_generation` | New-run reset → validated findings rendered in task order at exit → existing report-generation cleanup |\n64: \n65: **Ownership and run lifecycle**\n66: \n67: - At a new enabled main-graph research run, the brief-to-supervisor boundary clears the previous public run snapshot and notes with explicit overrides where required. An explicitly fresh supervisor input initializes its private counters/receipts at the supervisor entry; do not try to store supervisor-private keys in `AgentState` or infer a fresh run merely from `exit_reason is None`. A resume of that same run does not reinitialize them. There is one supervisor run per main invocation; a later invocation must not inherit its predecessor's ledger or progress. The fresh-versus-resumed subgraph input protocol is a required integration test, not a claimed baseline capability.\n68: - Every observed delegation intent is registered in original tool-call order before dispatch or final coverage selection. A valid spec receives the next run-local task ID. Invalid intent, including no valid topic/question or more than five distinct valid questions, produces a rejected diagnostic receipt, without inventing a spec or a coverage question. The definition of an accepted delegation and this diagnostic representation require shared-reader Review.\n69: - A receipt records the original call identity, accepted serialized spec/task ID or rejection diagnostic, and its eventual terminal result reference. Re-observing the same unchanged receipt does not create another task or append another result. A reused ID with different intent is a conflict diagnostic, not a second silently accepted task. An already terminal receipt is not converted to `not_run` on a cap resume.\n70: - Delivery produces dispatch inputs and one ordered result delta; it does not directly mutate graph state or choose an exit. The node's orchestration boundary validates that delta, updates receipts, and emits each new terminal result once. These are event responsibilities at one integration point, not two modules writing the same field.\n71: - While the supervisor runs, its ledger may be nonempty with `exit_reason is None`. This is a valid **running** state. `RunRecord.from_state()` is exclusively a reader of the final main snapshot and rejects an incomplete final snapshot; it is not called on the running supervisor ledger. The five final run fields are published together through one finalization update. Private counters, receipts and evidence snapshots are not main output fields.\n72: - Per-turn `update_payload[\"task_results\"]` is a plain delta: the reducer appends it. The coverage calculation may temporarily inspect `old_results + delta`, but must not return that full list as a plain update. At finalization, both `task_results` and `notes` use `{\"type\": \"override\", \"value\": ...}`. Notes contain only rendered validated claims and their validated source references, never the untrusted complete summary, JSON sidecar, error, placeholder or reflection.\n73: \n74: **Execution evidence and context**\n75: \n76: - Each accepted task gets one live `TaskExecutionEvidence` holder. The dispatch wrapper makes a separate copy of `RunnableConfig` and of its `configurable` mapping, then injects the holder under the runtime-private key `_orchestration_evidence`. Parallel tasks never share this holder. It is passed only to the orchestration execution adapters; strip that private key before constructing model configuration, prompt content or exported diagnostics. Do not put the live holder, locks, permits, callbacks, model clients or awaitables in graph state; state contains serializable snapshots only.\n77: - The wrapper owns the live holder for the entire invocation, including exceptional returns. Before starting local awaitables, it reserves ordered permits within the remaining actual-call budget; it records each started executable invocation, including `think_tool` and failures. Rejected calls and the non-executed `ResearchComplete` control signal spend zero. Factories `build_task_result`, `validate_task_result` and `failed_result` receive the holder through the required keyword-only `evidence` argument, so a model cannot submit authoritative cost or retrieved URLs. `not_run_result(spec)` uses zero-spend evidence by construction.\n78: - Spec, remaining budget, receipts and trusted retrieved URLs survive message trimming. Before every compression attempt, assembly restores the immutable spec/authority and the sidecar rules outside expendable history. A serializable evidence snapshot supports inspection/checkpoint validation, but does not turn a live external tool's side effects into a transaction.\n79: - Checkpoint or trace adapters must exclude the runtime-private holder itself. Receipt deduplication protects local registration/result publication, not cross-process exactly-once tool execution: a crash after an external call but before a terminal receipt is durable leaves a replay window. Safe recovery of an unfinished receipt requires an explicit policy; no automatic side-effect replay guarantee is made here.\n80: - Existing model `*_max_tokens` fields are output limits and `max_content_length` is a character limit. This design supplies no measured input/context-window token cap. Reactive compression token-limit handling, at most three attempts, remains the current trigger; proactive course input-budget compliance is a documented integration gap, not an implemented threshold.\n81: \n82: **Feature flags and shared-reader Review**\n83: \n84: | `orchestration_enabled` | `context_trimming` | Proposed lifecycle |\n85: |---|---|---|\n86: | `False` | `False` | Exact baseline path; no new orchestration fields, injected holder or altered tool binding. |\n87: | `True` | `False` | Task contract/evidence and typed result mapping apply; retain the baseline compression retry/trimming path rather than taking over context policy. Compression success is parsed and failure is mapped using retained evidence. |\n88: | `True` | `True` | Context exclusively owns the single recovery loop, at most three attempts; it returns its existing string outcome. Orchestration parses that string and maps `TokenLimitExceeded` through `failed_result(..., evidence=...)`; no nested retry loop. |\n89: | `False` | `True` | Context's exception can exist without a TaskSpec. Its propagation/delivery behavior is **pending context/delivery Review**; do not manufacture orchestration fields or claim this combination is the baseline. |\n90: \n91: - **Shared contract:** delivery/Alchemy reads or creates results through helpers; context/BroZhai (Tekon) maps compression failure through the same factory; citations/Jeremy reviews the optional machine-readable sidecar and protected prompt placement; evaluation/Doppler reads only final outputs. Reader approval is pending. Native search is rejected before a flag-on model request until strict pre-request tool caps and auditable provenance/counts are agreed; search's existing configuration validator and provider selection stay separate.\n92: - **Planned verification:** `test/offline/test_contracts.py::{test_task_results_reducer_append_and_override,test_run_record_final_snapshot_contract}` and `test/offline/test_orchestration_integration.py::{test_receipt_replay_does_not_duplicate_tasks,test_new_run_resets_private_progress,test_finalization_joins_then_replaces_notes_and_results,test_flag_combinations,test_enabled_context_assembly_preserves_authority}`.
