Nothing below is human-approved; the ADR is Proposed, and none of this claims runtime correctness.\n\n## Verdict\n**No blocker in the deterministic core** (question identity, coverage fraction, ordered selector, sidecar/source rules, delta/override protocol). The cross-references to INV-003/006/007, POL-002–010, ORC-001–042, §5 interfaces and §9 steps are internally consistent for the cases they enumerate. The findings below are ordering, budget-accounting and evidence gaps that would surface as an implementer's ambiguous choice or a failure-cost overclaim.\n\n## MUST-FIX (implementation blockers)\n\n**F1. The cap check runs before admission too late for the last turn, and the outer bound is too weak. [ADR §3.4, POL-007, ORC-025]**\nThe scenario check \"if count is already N, register but do not dispatch\" happens at tools entry. The enabled path then says \"process the turn, join, then increment and evaluate.\" So for N=1, exactly one processed turn \"may delegate\" as ADR claims, but ORC-025 only asserts \"no more than N turns,\" so it will not catch off-by-one dispatch at N+1. Decide one rule and write it: for N=1 the first delegation turn is admitted because the count was 0 at entry. Then add an explicit expected-dispatched-task-count column for N=1 and N=2. Also state the outer bound: the entry guard plus one in-turn increment can leave at most one model request in flight after the cap is reached. Without this, an implementer can loop one extra request on a think-only turn at cap.\n\n**F2. The compressed-research strip boundary is narrower than the compression prompt actually used. [ADR §3.3, §9.8, ORC-023, structural-constraints §10 Compression protocol]**\nBaseline `compress_research_prompt` (prompts.py L197–215) does not produce a \"findings\" schema: it asks for a comprehensive report plus \"### Sources\" with sequential numbers. Yet the ADR requires `<task_findings_v1>` JSON with `{\"claim\",\"source_numbers\",\"answers\"}`, and \"answer strings exactly matching `must_answer` items.\" The strip rule only covers \"the sidecar → before `### Sources`.\" That leaves the larger free-text report body that precedes the sidecar. Under the current wording, the adapter strips the sidecar, leaving the whole model report as `TaskResult.summary` and `compressed_research`. This conflicts with §3.3's \"raw context string cannot bypass this adapter,\" and ORC-023 only tests sidecar removal. Tighten ORC-023 and §3.3 to say the *entire* compression body except validated findings and the `### Sources` list is dropped. `summary` is then either `\"\"` or a code-generated string, never the model report body. This is not final-report citation validation (out of REQ-006 scope). It is about which task-level string reaches the supervisor.\n\n**F3. `max_tool_calls` uses `max_react_tool_calls` but that baseline field is a *turn* counter, and the ADR does not name the mapping failure. [ADR §3.2; configuration.py L107–119, deep_researcher.py L422/L492]**\n`max_react_tool_calls` is described as \"tool calling iterations\" and is incremented per researcher *turn* (L422), not per invocation. The ADR silently reinterprets it as a start-count maximum. The default of 10 invocations will be hit much sooner than today's 10 turns × batch size. That is a behaviour change to the enabled path that §3.2 acknowledges only obliquely (\"Initially use the configured `max_react_tool_calls` as that maximum\"). This needs a one-line consequence and a reviewer note, because readers will otherwise assume flag parity. Either add `task_max_tool_calls` as a separate config, or record that enabled tasks get materially tighter budgets and that C-006's comparability review is affected.\n\n**F4. Missing or adversarial usage metadata is not covered by the \"reported usage sums\" rule and can overstate or hide cost. [ADR §3.2, §8; ORC-016, INV-006]**\n§3.2 says \"token counters sum **reported** usage\" and that missing metadata is recorded as \"incomplete.\" It does not say how the `TaskCost` reader treats an incomplete receipt. The result is that ORC-016's \"failed task retains its trusted invocation/usage cost\" is only testable for the tool-call count, not tokens. Specify: `TaskCost.input_tokens/output_tokens` either (a) carry a sibling `usage_incomplete: bool`, or (b) the contract rejects a result whose receipt is marked incomplete but claims nonzero token cost. Right now, INV-006's \"recorded spending includes started invocations\" is true for the tool-call column only and is untested for the token columns.\n\n**F5. Supervisor-side non-executable calls aside from `ResearchComplete` lack a stated disposition. [ADR §3.2, §3.4]**\nThe ADR says: \"resolve all other calls in a mixed batch before compression.\" But the researcher tool registry also includes `ResearchComplete` and any MCP tools, and `get_all_tools` returns dict-shaped native-search entries that have no `.name`. ADR §3.2 says each `ResearchComplete` is \"a control signal with no executable invocation,\" but §3.4 says mixed completion/delegation turns \"process all valid delegations.\" These two conflict for the case where `ResearchComplete` arrives *with* a delegation at the last permitted turn. Also missing: a call naming an unregistered or unknown tool fails with what category? State one rule: unknown tool name = `invalid_result` or a typed rejection, and it costs zero because no invocation started. Otherwise ORC-017's \"mismatched ToolMessage id\" case has no expected disposition for a missing tool.\n\n## Reader approvals / declared gaps (not blockers, but blocking Gate 1)\n\n**F6. One owner for the sidecar and notes hunk is asserted but not named at the same place as the citations owner's format. [ADR §3.3, §9.7–8; memory-design flag table]** — §9.7 gives Centay \"the successful result adapter\"; §9.8 gives Jeremy \"the citations format\"; but §3.3 says \"final cross-task renumbering remains citations/report prompt's job,\" and memory-design says citations \"reviews the optional machine-readable sidecar.\" Then who strips the sidecar before the *report* prompt? Adding an explicit sentence resolves this: \"the citations owner consumes only `notes`; the sidecar never reaches `final_report_generation`.\" Otherwise the hunk-split is ambiguous about whether the report path can still see the raw model body from F2.\n\n**F7. The off/on flag cell says \"do not fabricate TaskSpec\" but does not say what `TaskResult` shape (if any) the delivery path expects. [ADR §6, POL-010, memory-design flag table]** — This is honestly declared as BLOCKED on a human policy decision, but the ADR should also state that the off/on `RunRecord.from_state()` is not called, or that it raises by design. Otherwise an implementer could read `from_state` contractually as \"returns None only when all fields absent.\" The current text does not say off/on always has all run fields absent.\n\n**F8. The ADR evaluates no implementation cost for the changed schema of `ConductResearchV2` beyond \"strict parsing can reject useful but malformed output.\" [ADR §8, EVAL-002 K6]** — §8 costs list model-behaviour risk but not the *supervisor prompt* change implied by `must_answer`/`scope` (new fields, one more tool-arg level). If the supervisor prompt (§9.8) adds required fields, the enabled path's model failure rate is a measurable cost not listed. This is not a blocker, but it should appear in \"Reopen this decision when…\" alongside native search.\n\n## Findings from EVAL-002 and cross-document consistency\n\n**F9. EVAL-002 K1 says \"no model value affecting either [status or exit_reason]\" — this is overclaim relative to §3.4. [evaluation-table.md L60]** — The selector uses `supervisor_error` as an *input* (POL-008) and counts think-only turns as non-progress (POL-007). A model can influence coverage only through validated findings, but \"no model value affecting either\" is stronger than what the ADR implements: model *turns* affect stall and cap. The claimed meaning of \"model self-declared status/exit is never accepted as authority\" is correct. K1's wording should be read with that qualification; as written it overstates determinism (a stray completion-only turn at N−1 consumes the cap and changes the exit from `covered` to `budget_exhausted`). Recommend K1 read: \"no model-declared status, exit or provenance value is accepted as authority; only validated findings can affect computed status or coverage.\"\n\n**F10. ORC-029 says \"threshold 0/NaN/>1\" but ADR §3.4 requires `Fraction(str(threshold))` with a finite check. [verification-constraints.md L120, ADR §3.4]** — The Fix list already covers \"`Fraction(str())` not `Fraction(float)`.\" The concrete addition here: ORC-029, and the eventual ADR wording, should explicitly include the case `coverage_threshold=0.1+0.2`-style binary float (which would otherwise be 0.30000000000000004 in `float`). Test the exact fraction path with a config value that would differ under `Fraction(float)`, for example a threshold of `0.3` where `Fraction(0.3)` is not `3/10`. Without that, the \"exact coverage\" claim in F9 has no adversarial test.\n\n## Suggested resolution order\nF1 → F2 → F4 before Gate 1, because they change what a test assertion means. F3 and F5 before implementation steps 4 and 6. F9 and F10 are one-line wording changes in EVAL-002 and ORC-029. F6–F8 are reviewer commentary; declare them as reader approvals, not as new blockers.\n\nAll cases in this review are **PLANNED / NOT_RUN**. The PASS10 reference is only a schema-validation result; none of the above asserts a runtime outcome.","time":{"start":1794598779617,"end":1794598794696}}}
